# Lessons learned

What each slice of [`detailed-journeys.md`](detailed-journeys.md) taught about building Telo Ride
faster, so the next slice starts where the last one finished. It is a working tool, not a diary: a
lesson earns its place only if it saves time or prevents a mistake in a later slice.

## How to use it

1. **Before a slice:** read the standing lessons and the latest log entry. Put the lessons that fit
   the slice in its todo list, and apply them as you go.
2. **After a slice, before it merges:** add its entry at the top of the log, in the shape below.
   - _Applied from earlier:_ each earlier lesson you applied and its result: **saved time**, **no
     effect**, **did not apply**, or **made it worse**, with a sentence on why.
   - _What slowed it down:_ the few things that cost the most time, plainly.
   - _New lessons:_ each one as what happened, then what to do next time, concrete enough to follow
     (a command, a file, a value).
3. **Keep the standing lessons current:** add each new lesson to the table, fill in _Applied in_
   and _Result_ for the ones you used, and remove a lesson that twice had no effect or that the
   code, a tool or `AGENTS.md` now makes automatic.

The same step updates **% complete** in [`detailed-journeys.md`](detailed-journeys.md) and
[`journeys.md`](journeys.md).

## Log

### 40.1 to 40.6 Ride it your way (2026-11-25)

Journey 40, all six slices: intensity from 0 to 10 on every ride (40.1), kept as the default when the rider says so on
Class Done (40.2), and in Ibiza the Sound button with Music and No music (40.3), then YouTube video over the course, the
class video in time with the ride, YouTube audio with the picture hidden, and the notice when YouTube cannot play (40.4
to 40.6, one commit: they share one store method and one spec). ADR 0050; the owner chose 10 % a notch, intensity on
both ways, and a hidden player against YouTube's policies.

**Applied from earlier**

- **L93 (a paused clock holds rendering):** saved time once read: two presses of + sent 6/10 twice until the spec
  stepped the clock between them (and L260 made the control safe anyway).
- **L118 (move a replaced control's specs in the same commit):** saved time. The Music button's spec became the Sound
  button's in 40.3, and nothing else used `ride.music`.
- **L250 (rerun a full run's failures alone):** saved time. Three failures of the full run were the environment's (L259).
- **L253 (a ride-starting helper counts the countdown out):** made it worse once: the helper also waits for the plan's
  first level, which a kept intensity scales; `startClassRide` now takes `firstLevel`.

**What slowed it down**

- The session's browser: Playwright's pinned Chromium was missing, and a full run took 7 minutes on 4 cores (L259).
- Slices written ahead of their commit: 40.2 and 40.3 shared five files with 40.1 and 40.4, and a `stash --keep-index`
  pop conflicted (L261).
- `--target-min` read as 44 px in both the mock and the component (L262).

**New lessons**

- **L259** to **L262**, in the table.

### 39.6 When the flyover falls short (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, sixth slice: a bike lost in flight holds the landing with Try again; a course that cannot be drawn gives way
to the countdown; reduced motion has no flyover; Cancel releases the course; flyover frames stay within the budgets.

- **What slowed it:** little: the store written in 39.5 already carried the rules, so this slice was its specs. A lost
  bike on Playwright's paused clock needs `switchOn` and a stepped clock (`bikeAnswers`), not `bikeBack`.
- **Outcome of open question 45:** the high camera (55 m up, 40 m behind, 60 m ahead) holds both budgets at the heaviest
  step, so the low-over-the-road fallback was not needed.

### 39.5 Ibiza Flyover (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, fifth slice: a ride in Ibiza opens on Ibiza Flyover (the course from above between a bar and a band, five
captions, Skip flyover, Cancel), lands on the grid and opens Class Ride on its countdown, with the course drawn once.

- **What slowed it:** moving one `CourseScene` between two screens. Taking its host view out of a `ViewContainerRef` in a
  directive's `onDestroy` is too late: the container has already destroyed its views, so the renderer was released as the
  flyover went. The scene now belongs to a session (`createComponent` + `ApplicationRef.attachView`) and only its element
  moves, to a comment node in the next screen, after that screen's first render.
- **Lesson:** a thing that outlives the screen that shows it is owned by a service, never by the screen's view container.
- **Lesson:** a spec helper that starts a ride is the one place to absorb a new screen in front of it (`skipFlyover`,
  `startRideInIbiza`); only specs that click Start by hand needed an edit.
- Full parallel runs still flake the Ibiza connect log under load (L250); rerun alone.

### 39.4 Roll on past the finish line (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, fourth slice: through the ending the course is placed by the class length plus the seconds since the finish,
so the rider rolls on down the road past the finish line (L2-174 AC7, L2-182 AC5).

**Applied from earlier**

- **L243 (step the paused clock in frames):** saved time. The spec jumps and draws a few frames each time.

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: the course's driver already carries the clock on between whole seconds while `running` is true, so the spec
  passes before and after the binding. It is kept as the guard that the rider does not stop at the line.

**New lessons**

- **L258:** a spec written first can pass for a slice whose behaviour an earlier slice already delivered; say so in the
  entry and keep it as a guard, rather than weakening it to make it fail.

### 39.3 Ease off and roll on (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, third slice: after the finish the target resistance steps down to level 4, 110 ms apart, the stepper is
off, and a roll-out card with a filling bar shows until Class Done opens by itself (L2-182).

**Applied from earlier**

- **L253, L255, L256:** saved time. Helpers were ready; the key was right; a spec that failed once was rerun alone.

**What slowed it down**

- The e2e project cannot import `@telo/*`: a spec that borrowed `easeOffLevels` from `domain` broke `tsc -p
e2e/tsconfig.json`; the spec keeps its own three-line copy.
- `no-inline-styles` forbids `[style.--value]` in a template; a component sets a custom property on its host instead.

**New lessons**

- **L257:** a spec that counts commands 110 ms apart steps the paused clock until the last one is seen, then compares;
  a fixed `runFor` is one timer short about one run in four.

### 39.2 Finish banner (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, second slice: at 20:00 a banner lies across the video's or the course's box with the output and time, the
clock holds, the record is written, and Class Done opens when the 8.4 s ending is over (L2-181).

**Applied from earlier**

- **L253 (a ride-starting helper counts the countdown out):** saved time. No spec needed to learn it again.
- **L250 (parallel runs flake):** saved time. Five end-of-ride specs failed in a full run and passed alone.

**What slowed it down**

- Every spec that waits for Class Done now waits through 8.4 s of ending; `ClassDonePage.shown()` steps the paused
  clock 500 ms at a time for it, so no spec changed its own steps.
- A spec asserted `telo-ride.ride-record` is null, a key that does not exist, so it could never fail.

**New lessons**

- **L255:** the ride record's storage key is `telo-ride.record` (`RIDE_RECORD_KEY`); check a "not written" assertion
  against a ride that does write it.
- **L256:** a full parallel e2e run on this machine fails a different dozen specs each time; trust a spec that passes
  alone, and judge a slice by the specs that fail the same way twice.

### 39.1 Countdown (2026-11-24)

Journey 39, first slice: Class Ride opens on "3, 2, 1, Go!"; the ride clock, the video and the music start on "Go!"
(L2-180).

**Applied from earlier**

- **L25, L234 (all-or-nothing edit scripts):** saved time. Every multi-file edit checked its anchors first.
- **L75, L243 (`runFor` and `fastForward` on a paused clock):** saved time. The countdown spec steps the paused clock.
- **L250 (a full parallel run flakes one or two Ibiza specs):** saved time. Two music specs failed once in the full run
  and passed alone, twice.

**What slowed it down**

- Every helper that starts a ride had to count the countdown out; five specs about the FTMS bike's 3 s answer and the
  video's start broke before they were taught it.
- A second Playwright run started while the first was still alive collided on port 4300 and wrote into one
  `test-results`; stop the first run's process tree before starting another.

**New lessons**

- **L253:** a helper that starts a ride counts the countdown out (`COUNTDOWN_MS`) so specs still begin at 0:00. An FTMS
  bike times its 3 s answer from the ride's start, so its helper stops at "3" and its specs count the countdown out.
- **L254:** the fake YouTube player starts at `playerVars.start`, as the real one does, so a video parked at 0:03 reads 3.

### 38.1 to 38.7 Diagnose a bike that misbehaves (2026-11-23)

Journey 38, all seven slices in one change: the diagnostic recording of L2-178, off unless the app is opened with
`?diagnose` (ADR 0048).

**Applied from earlier**

- **L1, L13 (own worktree, own `npm ci`, the verify stamp):** saved time. The main checkout held another session's
  uncommitted work on `fix-ex3-pedalling`; a worktree cut from `origin/main` never touched it.
- **L2 (`E2E_PORT`):** saved time. Every run used 4380.
- **L5, L54, L72, L77 (read the initial total after each step):** saved time, twice. It showed the CDK dialog's cost
  before anything was designed on it (L247), and it showed `main` at 243.06 kB after every slice.
- **L17 (judge verify by its exit status):** saved time.
- **L18, L57 (bind on the lazy parent route):** saved time. The recorder, the tap and the recording's logger are bound
  there, so one recording serves every screen and none of it is in `app.config.ts`.
- **L19 (`*.development.spec.ts`):** saved time. The simulated bike's recording is proved in the development project.
- **L25, L234 (all-or-nothing edit scripts):** saved time. Every multi-file edit went through one script that checks
  each anchor occurs once before it writes anything; four edits missed their anchor and none left a file half changed.
- **L27, L29 (frames after initialisation; the keepalive joins every log):** saved time for the first specs, and L29 was
  still forgotten once (L251).
- **L41, L69 (see the spec fail first):** saved time. The spec for "off" failed for a reason nobody planned (L246).
- **L43 (the paused clock stops change detection):** saved time. Specs resume the clock before pressing the mark.
- **L63 (an attribute button takes its name as content):** applied late. The mark first drew its own word, and lint
  refused the empty button.
- **L34 (render every diagram):** did not apply to this session's own files; the designs' diagrams were rendered by
  the skill's script.

**What slowed it down**

- **The recording's code was in every rider's chunks, twice.** First through the libraries' main entry points (L246),
  caught by the spec for "off"; then through the service worker's prefetch (L248), caught by reading
  `ngsw-config.json` while writing the deployment note, after every spec had passed.
- **The dialog.** `AGENTS.md` asks for a CDK Dialog, and the CDK left the workspace with ADR 0043. Measuring it took
  one build and settled it: the platform's `<dialog>`, recorded as a departure in ADR 0048.
- **The shell.** Long heredocs failed to parse in Git Bash, and the format check failed on untouched files until
  their line endings were rewritten (L249).
- **Specs that fail on `main`.** One Ibiza clock spec failed alone and was fixed in its own commit; others time out
  only under a full parallel run (L250).

**New lessons**

- L246 to L252, in the table above.
- **What was not run:** the heap check with the recording on (`e2e/heap/recording.heap.ts`) and the sweeps were written
  or left as they were and not run, as `AGENTS.md` asks. No recording has been made on a real bike: every byte in the
  specs came from the fake Bluetooth stack or the simulated bike, and the Screen Capture API was answered by a fixture.
  The first recording of a real Echelon EX-3 is a person's to make.

### 36.5 Page not found (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: the page, its words, the header with the wordmark and Full screen, and "Back to the class" were all
  specified (`not-found.spec.ts`). The one gap was L2-145 AC2's "the app opens as in L2-167" for a rider with no bike:
  a case now shows "Back to the class" leading to Connect Bike. It passed first run; no product code changed.

**New lessons**

- "As in" another requirement means both of its branches: here the bike remembered and not.

### 36.4 Theme and full screen (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Dark and light from the first paint, the text contrast of every role, and Full screen on every header (present or
  absent) were already covered (`theme.spec.ts`, `full-screen-everywhere.spec.ts`). The gap was L2-143 AC3, gold only
  for a new best: `theme-default-and-gold.spec.ts` counts elements drawn in the theme's gold on every screen but Class
  Done, and on Class Done for a new best (some) and for a ride that is not one (none). No product code changed.
- **L2-143 AC1 (no preference is dark) cannot be measured in Chromium:** emulating "no preference" still matches the
  light query, as `theme.spec.ts` already notes. My first attempt asserted it and failed on that, so it was removed
  rather than weakened; the rule is in the stylesheet (dark unless the device asks for light).

**New lessons**

- A test the browser cannot express belongs in the lessons as a gap, not in the suite as a skip.

### 36.3 Screen readers, contrast and motion (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Announcements, reduced motion and the efforts' colour-free states were already covered
  (`screen-readers-and-motion.spec.ts`, the Class Done specs). New `headings-and-timers.spec.ts`: one `h1` on each of
  the five screens (L2-141 AC1), every `timer` outside a live region (AC2), the resistance profile one named image with
  no list items (AC5). No product code changed.
- Two first-draft assertions were wrong, not the app: Class Done's verdict is not always "Class done." ("Your best is
  still 342 kJ"), and Class Ride has more than one `timer` (the clock and the segment countdown), so the check runs
  over all of them.
- **Contrast (L2-141 AC4) in both themes is not asserted here:** it is a sweep, run only when a person asks. Hover
  150 ms and panels 200 ms (L2-142 AC1) are the design tokens' values, not tested.

**New lessons**

- Read the requirement's plural before asserting a count: "Timers use `role=timer`" meant every timer.

### 36.2 Touch and keyboard (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- 44 px and 48 px targets and a visible focus ring were already covered (`touch-and-keyboard.spec.ts`). The gaps were
  L2-140 AC3 (no hover effect on text) and AC4 (a "Skip to content" link first on every page), now
  `skip-and-hover.spec.ts` for all five screens. No product code changed.
- The first draft pressed Tab and expected the link focused; on Class Ride, reached by in-app navigation, focus is
  already inside the page, so Tab went elsewhere. The spec now asserts the link is the first focusable element in the
  page's order and that Enter moves focus into `main`: what "when a page opens" means for a page opened by a link.
- Hover on text that is off-screen cannot be tried, so those elements are skipped.

**New lessons**

- Say whether an a11y spec is about a page loaded fresh or reached by navigation; the focus starting point differs.

### 36.1 Fit every width (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- The carried-over `fit-every-width.spec.ts` already covered no sideways scroll at 320 and 2560 px on every screen
  and the 16/24/32 px margins. The gap was "nothing is cut off": a page can fit its width while an element's box
  reaches outside it. `AppDocument.cutOff()` lists visible elements whose box leaves the window, and each screen's case
  now asserts it is empty. It passed at once, after skipping `.visually-hidden` live regions, which sit off-screen on purpose.
  No product code changed. "Nothing overlaps" is left to the responsive sweep (`e2e/sweeps/`), run on request.

**New lessons**

- A layout check that skips visually-hidden elements must say so; the first run flagged the live announcer on every screen.

### 35.2 Treat bike and stored data as untrusted (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the two 64-character unit tests failed on the lengths (100 for 64) before the cut.

**What slowed it down**

- A search for "64" found nothing: the cut to 64 characters (L2-129 AC2) had not been carried over, though the markup
  case had. `cutName` in `bike-identity.ts` now cuts every name the app shows (a device's name or id, an Echelon
  model's) and the name the remembered-bike store saves and reads. The rest was already in place and tested: names as
  text, a malformed record or bike read as absent, malformed frames ignored, and no per-packet console line.
- Unit tests added for malformed remembered-bike records (not an object, no id, a number for the id, bad JSON).

**New lessons**

- For each AC of a carried-over slice, search for its distinguishing number or word before assuming it was carried.

### 35.1 Restrict third parties (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- The carried-over policy still named `tile.googleapis.com` (Telo Ride's maps) in the page's and the workers' policies
  and had no `media-src`, so the music fell back to `default-src`. Both are fixed in
  `staticwebapp.config.json`; `static-host.spec.ts` now reads the policy's directives and fails for any host but
  `www.youtube.com`, `www.youtube-nocookie.com` and `i.ytimg.com`.
- **Order:** the config was fixed before the new spec was written, so the spec was never seen red. A policy test should
  be run against the old file first.
- `blob:` stays in `connect-src`, `img-src` and `worker-src` for the course's loader: same-origin by nature, and
  not tested against a real browser's CSP refusals.

**New lessons**

- Carried-over host config keeps its old third parties; grep it for every host, not only for what the slice adds.

### 34.4 Web vitals and readouts (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- LCP and INP on The Class were already a perf check (`home-vitals.perf.ts`). New: `class-ride-readouts.spec.ts`, a
  behaviour spec for L2-134 (a bike notification in Power and Cadence within 250 ms; the clock and the segment
  countdown move once a second), which passed on its first run; and `perf/ride-frames.perf.ts` for L2-136 AC3 (no
  long animation frame over 100 ms in a minute of riding at 4× CPU slowdown on a 1194 × 834 screen).
- **The frames check is written and type-checked, not run:** perf checks run only when a person asks (AGENTS.md). Its
  4× slowdown stands in for "the reference tablet" and has not been calibrated; run `npm run perf`.

**New lessons**

- The harness's paused clock gives exact timings for readouts, but a frame-time check needs the real clock, so it
  belongs with the perf checks, not the behaviour specs.

### 34.3 Heap checks (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- The two heap checks carried over measured a 20:00 ride and six rounds in Ibiza only. They now match L2-138: a ride
  left paused for three hours (the clock run in ten-minute steps, the bike still notifying) read at minute 1 and at
  the end against 50 MB, and The Class to Class Ride ten times, with the class video and in Ibiza, read from the third
  return to the tenth against 5 MB.
- **Written and type-checked, not run:** AGENTS.md has heap checks run only when a person asks, so neither the
  paused-ride check nor the ten-round check has been run since the rewrite. Run `npm run heap` before relying on them;
  the 3-hour check's step size and the warm-up round are guesses until it has.

**New lessons**

- A check an agent may not run is a claim, not evidence: say so in the slice's entry and the PR.

### 34.2 Running totals only (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: `RideRecorder` holds three numbers and `EffortTally` one total per effort; nothing else in the engine or the
  ride store pushes per second. No product code changed. Two unit tests now prove it: after four hours the recorder's
  own keys are the same three numbers, and the tally has the same one-per-effort shape after the plan and an hour more.

**New lessons**

- "Holds no trace" is testable as "own keys and sizes are the same after N seconds as after one".

### 34.1 Bundle budgets (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: the 300 KB gzip limit is enforced by `npm run bundle:size` in verify and CI (79.27 kB now), every route is
  lazy, and the renderer's laziness was already specified. The gap was the video player: a new spec shows `/connect`
  and the not-found page fetch none of its code and The Class does. `ScriptLog.hasVideoPlayerCode` looks for the embed
  host's name, which the minifier keeps. It passed on its first run; no product code changed.

**New lessons**

- When a requirement says "only where X shows", the spec needs a positive case (it does arrive on the screen that
  shows it) as well as the negatives, or a changed marker string passes silently.

### 33.2 Ride without a network (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- The class opening and starting offline was already specified and tested (`app-cache.spec.ts`); the new spec for a
  ride that loses the network mid-ride found a real defect: Class Done's lazy chunks were first fetched when the ride
  ended, so with the network gone the ride reached 20:00 and never left Class Ride. Fix: the `ride` route fetches
  `class-done.routes` and `class-done-page` with the ride (a failed fetch is ignored; the router retries at the end).
  The first attempt preloaded only the routes file, then only the page; both are needed.

**New lessons**

- A lazy route a ride ends in must be fetched before the ride can lose its network: test "network lost mid-flow" for
  every screen the flow ends in, not only the screens it starts at.
- With no service worker in a spec, `context.setOffline(true)` is a faithful model of the first visit; the worker
  only covers later ones.

### 33.1 Keep the ride clock true (2026-11-23)

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: the monotonic clock, the wake lock (taken again on visible, released at the end) and the leave prompt
  were carried over from Telo Ride with their specs (`ride-clock-hidden-tab`, `class-pause-leave`). The one gap was
  L2-016 AC2: keepalives in a throttled hidden tab, now `keepalive-hidden-tab.spec.ts`, which passed on its first run
  because the behaviour already held. No product code changed.
- L2-133 AC2's output is not asserted separately: the output is summed from the same monotonic seconds as the clock.

**New lessons**

- Before building a carried-over slice, list which of its ACs already have a passing spec; here only one lacked one.

### 32.7 A ride that could not be saved (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **Mock first:** the not-saved mock was drawn and registered before the spec; the spec failed on the missing notice.
- **L41 see the spec fail first:** two of three cases failed for the right reason, the third (saved) passed already.

**What slowed it down**

- 32.6's "Ride it again" broke every spec that seeds a record and then expects "Start class" in a shared fixture;
  found only because this slice reran neighbours. The fixture now accepts either label.

**Decisions**

- **`saved` rides in the result,** set from `RideRecord.add`'s return, so Class Done needs no storage access.

**New lessons**

- A slice that changes a label shared by fixtures must grep `e2e/fixtures` for that label, not just its own specs.

### 32.6 Come back to beat it (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the view's new cases were written before `record` and the three-argument
  `startAction` existed; the e2e spec failed on the missing figures.
- **Name the files Prettier writes:** only the slice's files were formatted.

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing blocked: the record (32.1), the mock's classes and the design system's rules were all there; the stats rules
  were ported from `components.css` to the component's own stylesheet.

**Decisions**

- **The record is read when the view is drawn,** not injected into the component: `wz-the-class` stays presentational and
  the store passes the snapshot, so a return from Class Done shows the new ride without a reload.
- **"Ride it again" is a third argument of `startAction`,** so the rule that a missing bike beats it stays in one place.

**New lessons**

- None new; the rule "a presentation component takes inputs" held and made this slice a view change plus markup.

### 32.5 On target (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — partly:** the view's `onTarget` case was written with the field in one step; the
  on-target spec's first case needed the new fact.
- **32.2's lesson on re-homing specs:** the old Results spec's "40 of 1,200 seconds is 3%" case, removed with the
  screen, is back here against Class Done, and a second case proves a ride with no power is 0%.

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: the share was already counted and unit-tested in `RideRecorder` (L2-037 AC3); the slice only shows it.

**Decisions**

- **The fact comes from the result's share, rounded in the view,** not recounted.

**New lessons**

- When a screen is replaced, list the old screen's specs' cases before deleting them and re-home each: 32.2 dropped
  two without noticing.

### 32.4 The eight efforts (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the view's cases failed to compile on `efforts`; the efforts spec was written with
  the component and shown red by the missing bars. The tally's own unit spec was written and implemented together,
  so it never failed.
- **Name the files Prettier writes (32.2) and use the Write tool for text with curly quotes (L-heredoc):** the first
  spec file went into a heredoc and broke the shell; the second time it went through the Write tool.

**What slowed it down**

- The slice's name says eight efforts; the plan has 17 and L2-169 already said so (its "mock gap" was the mock's eight).
  The row keeps its name; the screen, specs and mocks say 17.
- The app's own token file lacks tokens the design system has: `--duration-stagger` (the delay was 0 s), then
  `--class-done-chart-height` (the chart had no height until it was added). Check a token exists in
  `projects/ride/src/styles/tokens.css`, not only in `docs/design-system/tokens/`.
- With a real chart height the figures took room from the bars and skewed their ratio: the bar is now its share of
  the room under the figure.
- A bar still rising is scaled and fades in: specs read `offsetHeight`, not the bounding box.

**Decisions**

- **Upright figures** so 17 fit at 320 px without overlap, rather than hiding them or scrolling sideways.
- **The tally sums, and the view shapes:** `EffortTally` and `effortBars` are pure and unit-tested; the component only
  draws.

**New lessons**

- A token that exists in the mocks' design system must be checked in the app's token file before a component depends on it.

### 32.3 Best output (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the view's cases failed to compile on `best`; the verdict spec failed on the old
  hidden heading.
- **Name the files Prettier writes (32.2):** only the files of the slice were formatted; nothing else was touched.

**What slowed it down**

- The ride adds itself to the record before Class Done opens, so Class Done cannot read "the best before" from the
  record: the finish reads it first and carries it in the result.
- A `sed` that wrote a regex lost its backslash and `/.$/` cut the last letter of the verdict: edit with the script
  tool, not `sed`, when a pattern holds a backslash.

**Decisions**

- **The verdict is the view's, from whole kJ:** 342.4 kJ against a best of 342 is not a new best (L2-077 compares
  "whole kJ").
- **The announcement reads the verdict then the number,** so a screen reader hears the news first.

**New lessons**

- A screen that compares with "before" needs "before" in its input: a result carried in history state outlives the
  record it was compared with.

### 32.2 Class Done (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the view's unit spec and the Class Done spec were written before the screen, and
  failed on the missing module and the missing `/done` route.
- **Docs are not formatted by Prettier:** the mocks were edited with `sed` on three exact strings.
- **Asserted batches (L25):** the doc edits went in one all-or-nothing script.

**What slowed it down**

- `prettier --write` over `e2e`, `projects/ride/src/app` and `projects/components/src` marked 200 files as modified
  on this CRLF checkout (stat only: `git diff` showed nothing). Name the files you changed.
- Eleven specs named `ResultsPage`, `/results` or the Results texts; one scripted pass moved them, and the specs that
  read a "share on target" line now read the output and the average, because that share is slice 32.5's.
- A "one link" assertion counted the skip link: asserted within `main`.

**Decisions**

- **A presentation component for the whole screen (`wz-class-done`)** with `end` and `actions` slots, and the page
  (router, announcer, Full screen) in the app; the old Results code and its header were removed with the route.
- **A hidden "Class done" heading** until slice 32.3's verdict takes the `h1`.

**New lessons**

- Run Prettier on named files only on this checkout; `git diff --stat` before staging shows what it really touched.

### 37.9 One record whichever way (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — could not:** the behaviour came with 32.1, which adds the finished ride in
  `ClassRideStore.finish()` for either way; the acceptance spec passes on its first run and says so here.
- **37.5's lesson on cores — saved time:** the course's files are held and nothing is drawn.

**What slowed it down**

- Nothing: it is one spec. It asserts the device keeps one record of three fields and no key of Ibiza's own, then
  opens the app with the bike forgotten.

**Decisions**

- **No code:** the record never knew the way a ride was ridden, so there is nothing to remove.

**New lessons**

- When a slice only proves a rule an earlier slice made true, write its spec anyway and record that it never failed:
  it guards the rule against the next change.

### 32.1 Keep the record (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first:** the store's unit spec failed on a missing module; the acceptance spec was written
  after the store, and its first case would fail on the old stub, which always said "no finished ride".
- **37.5's lesson on cores — saved time:** two cheap specs, no course drawn.

**What slowed it down**

- The paused clock does not draw The Class by itself after a reload: the spec moves it on while it polls.
- `bluetooth.remember` is an init script that sets the bike on every load; the spec adds a second one after it that
  removes it, so only the record can tell a returning rider from a new one.

**Decisions**

- **One value, three integers:** `{rides, best, last}` under `telo-ride.record`, in whole kJ; anything else it holds
  reads as no rides (L2-168 AC5, open question 22: the ride-with choice stays apart).
- **The record is seen by where the app opens** until slice 32.6 shows it on The Class.
- **`FINISHED_RIDES` is bound with `useExisting` to `RIDE_RECORD`;** the guard did not change and `NoFinishedRides`
  went. Diagram sources were edited, their PNGs not re-rendered.
- **A ride is added when it finishes,** before Results opens; the notice for a record that cannot be written is
  slice 32.7 (`add` already returns false).

**New lessons**

- A stub a later slice replaces is cheapest when its consumer already depends on a contract: here the guard and its
  spec did not change.

### 37.8 Leave Ibiza clean (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — partly:** the spec first failed on a reading, not a leak: three's
  `WebGPURenderer.dispose()` is async and zeroes its own counts, so a count read just after it is still the count before.
- **37.7's lesson on measuring at many places — not needed:** one leave, one place.

**What slowed it down**

- Reading `info.memory` right after `dispose()` showed 1 geometry and 3 textures and looked like a leak. Reading three's
  source (`Renderer.dispose` awaits and then calls `info.dispose()`) showed they are the renderer's own.

**Decisions**

- **The report is a before and a drop,** not a zero: `heldBefore` greater than `held`, and `held` no more than the
  renderer's own (1 geometry, 3 textures).
- **AC4 and AC5 are not behaviour specs:** they need the reference tablet and a full ride; the heap and vitals checks
  exist and run on request, so the row is complete when the rule they prove is written and the leave is proved.

**New lessons**

- Read a library's `dispose` before measuring after it: an async dispose can make a count look wrong in either direction.

### 37.7 The course's budgets (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — saved time:** the budget spec failed on a real number: 130,130 triangles at the
  finish, over the 120,000 L2-177 AC1 allows. The course had never been measured at every place.
- **37.5's lesson on cores — saved time:** one software-drawn spec, one worker, one window; the sizes are asserted
  without drawing.

**What slowed it down**

- Six places along the course was too few: 119,275 triangles at the first fix passed there and would have failed
  between the samples. The spec samples 25 places, every 50 s and the last at 1190 s.

**Decisions**

- **The remedy is the map's draw distance, not its models:** `DRAWN_FAR` is 0.75 for the top three steps. Draw
  calls were never close (at most 85 of 140).
- **The step rule left the renderer for `FrameBudget`,** a pure class with unit tests, so AC3's 90 frames, 1.1 and
  1.6 are proved without a GPU.
- **"No texture on the map" is checked on the model file** (no `textures` or `images` in its glTF), and "no shadow
  map" on the renderer's own report. Procedural canvases the renderer makes (surface detail) are not on the map.

**New lessons**

- A budget is proved at its worst step and at many places: pin the heaviest step and sample the whole course.

### 37.6 The Music button (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — saved time:** the three button specs failed on a button that was not there.
- **L242 take the rule away — did not need it:** the specs asserted the pause, the unpressed state, the nothing-fetched
  case and the disabled case, and each failed first.
- **37.5's lesson on cores — saved time:** the button specs run with the course's files held and nothing drawn; the
  layout specs were run with `--workers=2`, not the whole suite (memory was short and the full run was not repeated).
- **L3, L25, L17 — saved time:** format first, asserted batches, exit statuses.

**What slowed it down**

- Windows `glob` returns backslash paths: the mock batch loaded the five Ibiza mocks again from disk under a second
  key and overwrote its own edits. The batch asserted nothing wrote half, but it wrote the wrong half; `git checkout --
docs` and a rerun with `.replace("\\", "/")` fixed it.
- The first test setup assumed `ibiza.html` had no button when the derived mocks were built from it; the batch's
  assertion caught it before anything was written.

**Decisions**

- **The button is in the corner of the course's box, not in the header:** the header already hides its words below
  384 px, and the music belongs to the course. It is a raised `wz-button` with `min-width` and `min-height` of 44 px,
  `aria-pressed`, and a stroke through its note when off or unavailable, so colour is never the only carrier.
- **A music that is off fetches nothing:** `play()` does nothing while the setting is off; the store plays only while
  `running() && music.on()`.
- **Unavailable is per ride:** the element's `error` event or a refused `play()` sets it, releasing the element
  resets it, and nothing alerts (L2-176 AC7).
- **The setting is one localStorage value, `telo-ride.music`,** written only as "off"; storage that is refused leaves
  the music on for the visit.

**New lessons**

- A mock batch must key files by one spelling of their path: normalise `glob` results before comparing them with
  paths built by hand.

### 37.5 Music (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41 see the spec fail first — saved time:** four of the five music specs failed on "no request for /music/" and
  "no audio element"; the fifth failed on its own setup (a second visit to The Class) and was split in two.
- **L35 check what exists — saved time:** nothing existed (no audio code, no file in `public/`), so the slice built
  the whole path; the course's deep house track had gone with 37.1.
- **L3, L25, L17 — saved time:** format first, one asserted doc batch, results read from exit status.
- **L43 a paused clock stalls things — did not apply:** the specs run on the real clock with the course held.

**What slowed it down**

- A shell heredoc with `’` and quotes in a TypeScript file failed with "unexpected EOF"; the files were written
  with the Write tool instead.
- The full suite flaked: `class-ride-ibiza.spec.ts` failed at `connectBike`, before any ride, in 1 of 3 full runs,
  and passed 12 of 12 with `--workers=2`. Each spec that draws the course in software takes several cores; 37.4's
  offline spec was the fifth. It was changed to assert that the worker served the three files with no network and
  to leave, not to draw a second time (the drawing is proved by `class-ride-ibiza.spec.ts`), and given 90 s.
- The second click of the Pause test used `ride.pause`, a locator named "Pause"; after pausing the button is
  "Resume" (`ride.resume`).

**Decisions**

- **The contract is `RideMusic` (`play`, `pause`, `release`) with token `RIDE_MUSIC`;** `BrowserRideMusic` is in
  `domain` beside the wake lock and is bound on the class ride route.
- **One `HTMLAudioElement` with `loop`,** not a decoded buffer (about 44 MB of PCM held for a ride, against
  the memory rule). Whether it loops with no gap is open question 32.
- **The store plays and pauses it from `running()` in an `effect`,** only in Ibiza; `release()` runs on finish and
  on destroy. The probe is an init script that records `Audio` elements; the app is not told.
- **The mp3 is `frontend/projects/ride/public/music/beautiful-boom-bap.mp3`,** copied unchanged from
  `docs/videos/assets/music/`; its size budget is slice 37.7's.

**New lessons**

- A spec that draws the course in software costs several cores: before adding one, count the ones there are
  (`grep -l "course.pin" e2e/*.spec.ts`), and prove a rule about files or the network without drawing. A failure
  in `connectBike` with nothing written to the bike is the sign of a busy machine, not of a bug.
- To see what a page does with `Audio`, wrap `window.Audio` in an init script; Playwright's headless Chromium plays
  the mp3 and advances `currentTime` on the real clock.

### 37.4 When Ibiza cannot be drawn (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L41, L242 see the spec fail first, and take the rule away — saved time:** the four cannot-draw specs failed on the
  missing notice, and the offline spec failed at the second ride until the worker kept the files.
- **L3 format first; L25 asserted batches; L35 check what exists — saved time:** `CourseScene` already emitted
  `unavailable`; only the host, the store, the box and the worker's list were missing.
- **L43 a paused clock stalls the first frame — saved time once learned:** the first cannot-draw spec installed a
  clock and waited for the notice, which never came; the cases run on the real clock.

**What slowed it down**

- `CourseSceneHost` re-emitted `drawn` and nothing else, so `(unavailable)` on it was silently a DOM event. It took
  three instrumented runs to see it. An output bound on a directive that does not declare it is not an error.
- The first full-doc Prettier run of slice 37.3 had taught that `docs/` is not formatted; this slice did not repeat it.

**Decisions**

- **The notice is `wz-inline-message` under the box,** as the class video's is, with `class-course__notice`.
- **A lost context has 3 seconds** to come back (`CONTEXT_GRACE_MS`); then the course is given up for the ride.
- **WebGPU device loss is not watched** (open question 31): the app runs WebGL 2 where WebGPU is absent, and no
  device-lost path could be tried here.
- **AC4 and AC5 are the worker's job:** `/courses/**` and `/riders/**` join the lazy `assets` group of
  `ngsw-config.json`. The spec proves them together: a second ride with the network gone draws the course.

**New lessons**

- Bind an output on `CourseSceneHost` only after declaring it there; a spec that asserts the page's change is the
  only thing that catches a missing re-emit.
- A spec about a failing load does not need Playwright's clock: leave it real and wait on the notice.

### 37.3 The course as built (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L3 format first; L17 judge verify by its exit status — saved time:** Prettier before each run; verify's exit
  status decides the commit.
- **L41 see the spec fail before the code — saved time:** the acceptance spec failed with the course drawn from a
  haze of `sunset` (the page held the canvas and not the still), and 26 new unit rows failed because `parseCourse`
  did not throw, before either was made to pass.
- **L35 check what earlier slices built — saved time:** the length rule, the grid and the finish arch already held
  in the shipped file and in `ibiza.glb`; only the gate on what a file names was missing.
- **L25 asserted batches — saved time:** the doc edits went in one asserted batch.
- **L1 own checkout with `npm ci` — did not apply as written:** this checkout had no `node_modules`, so `npm ci`
  ran in place on the branch.

**What slowed it down**

- `ng test ride --include` takes a path relative to the `ride` project (`../../world/src/course.spec.ts`), not to
  `frontend/`; the first run found no tests.
- `npx prettier --write` on `docs/` reformatted whole tables; `format:check` covers `frontend/` only, so the docs
  were restored and the edits made again.
- On this Windows checkout `core.autocrlf=true` leaves CRLF in the working tree and `verify`'s format step fails on
  files nobody touched (`tsconfig.json` among them); verify stops at that step, so lint, unit tests, the build and
  the specs were run one by one.

**Decisions**

- **L2 open question 18: AC3 takes the built value** (0.855 % a level with ramps); the course is not rebuilt.
- **L2-175 AC6 is enforced at `parseCourse`,** not in the look helpers: a sky, haze, light, fill, lamps, lights, rain
  or wet the file names wrongly makes the course malformed; what it leaves out is the sunset's. The helpers'
  fallbacks stay, unreachable for a parsed file.
- **The stages must lie end to end** (seconds and metres, level 1 to 32, first at the grid, last at the course's
  length) within half a second and half a metre.

**New lessons**

- To run one `world` spec: `npx ng test ride --watch=false --include='../../world/src/<name>.spec.ts'`.
- Never run Prettier on `docs/`; check `git diff --stat` after any formatter.

### 37.2 Placed by the clock (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L2, L204 the port — saved time:** every spec, verify and recording run used `E2E_PORT=4377`; the server started
  for the film was stopped by its process id, and nothing was killed by name or port.
- **L3 format first; L17 judge verify by its exit status — saved time:** verify exited 0 before the first edit, after
  the code, before the film and before the commit.
- **L164, L165 verify first; counts from four commands — saved time:** 331 specs, 51 `@critical`, 601 unit tests and
  79.19 kB before; 334, 52, 621 and 79.19 kB after.
- **L178, L200 say what the carried-over code already met — saved time:** all of it. The 3 new specs and the
  strengthened one passed as soon as the renderer's report carried `frames` and `camera`: the clock already placed
  the rider, a held ride already held them, a hidden tab already drew nothing, the camera already closed in, and the
  figures were already the same either way.
- **L213 see a spec fail after its code — saved time, and became L242:** each rule was taken out in turn. Placed by
  the bike's speed: three specs failed, at 0 m where 199.5 m were expected. Drawing while hidden: frame 13 where
  frame 6 was expected. The camera never closing in, and the loop running while paused: both passed, and the specs
  were given a reading inside the range and a count of frames, after which they failed at 2.75 m and at frame 755.
- **L223 ride the course in the smallest window — saved time:** three specs draw the course, each once, at
  844 × 390, pinned at its lowest step; the two new ones take about 10 s each. Verify's specs took 45.2 s,
  from 40.9 s.
- **L239 pause the clock well ahead — did not apply to the new specs:** their clock is paused before the start
  button (L244). The holds spec keeps its ten seconds.
- **L190 repeated runs under `CI=1` — saved time:** `--grep @critical --repeat-each 3`, 156 of 156; the two files of
  this slice `--repeat-each 10`, 40 of 40. An earlier run of the holds spec six times had failed once (L243)
  before its waits were put back.
- **L25, L227, L234 batches from a file, written at the end — saved time:** nine doc edits in four files at the
  first run.
- **L150, L196, L218, L229, L236 the film — saved time:** ten scenes, thirteen pictures in the film;
  CRF 23 gave 15.7 MB for 2:25, over the limit, and CRF 25 gave 12.9 MB.
- **L28 frames to a new folder — skipped once:** the scratchpad's `frames` folder held another session's pictures;
  only the names kept them apart.
- **L154, L172 the mock beside the app — did not apply:** nothing new is drawn on the page.
- **L147, L148 — did not apply:** nothing was removed.
- **L216 — did not apply:** `origin/main` had not moved.**

**What slowed it down**

- Two specs passed with their rule taken away (L242).
- Seconds crossed with `fastForward` after Resume left the clock where it was, 1 run in 6 (L243).
- L2-174 AC10's numbers were not the code's (L245).

**Decisions**

- **AC10 takes the built numbers (L2 open question 30):** the camera sits 2.75 m behind and 1.8 m above the rider
  and closes in to 2.4 m and 1.65 m, eased over 45 m either side of the grid and 70 m either side of the finish
  line. The criterion, the slice's prompt and its row had the course's first numbers (4.9 m, 2.25 m, 3.5 m,
  1.8 m). The picture was not changed; the prompt's file still says 4.9 m.
- **The camera's rule is a pure function** (`world`'s `chase-camera.ts`), moved out of the renderer with 17 unit
  tests, both sides of 45 m and of 70 m. The placement stays `Course.metresAt`, whose table gained three rows.
- **"At that second"** is read as never behind the clock's second and never more than one ahead: the course is
  drawn between two seconds. Written into open question 30.
- **The test hook's report gained `frames` and `camera`.** Nothing else changed in product code, and nothing in how
  the course looks.
- **AC5 is proved in the holds spec**, which already paused the ride and lost the bike in Ibiza, rather than in a
  third ride of the course; it now also brings the bike back. `bikeAnswers` moved from `class-pause-leave.spec.ts`
  to `fixtures/class-ride.ts`, unchanged but for the level it reports.
- **AC4 is one spec with the course's files held:** forty seconds on target and then nothing, in Ibiza and then with
  the class video on the same page, giving the same "9 kJ · 3% on target" and 20:00. The efforts are not compared:
  no screen shows them until slice 32.4.
- **No ADR and no mock:** nothing architectural was decided and nothing new is drawn.
- **Not done:** the design's diagrams were not redrawn; none names what changed.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- Deleted: none. Weakened: none.
- New: `class-ride-ibiza-clock.spec.ts`, 3 specs, one `@critical`.
- Changed in substance: `class-ride-ibiza-holds.spec.ts`, strengthened (the rider's metre while paused and with the
  bike lost, the frames drawn while paused, and the ride carried on from the same metre after each).
  `class-pause-leave.spec.ts` imports the helper it used to hold.

### 37.1 The course in the video's box (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L2, L204 the port — saved time:** every spec, verify and recording run used `E2E_PORT=4377`; the two servers
  started for the film were stopped by their process ids, and nothing was killed by name or port.
- **L3 format first; L17 judge verify by its exit status — saved time:** verify exited 0 before the first edit, after
  the code, before the first commit and before the last.
- **L164, L165 verify first; counts from four commands — saved time:** 336 specs, 51 `@critical` and 79.29 kB
  before; 331, 51, 601 unit tests and 79.19 kB after. The unit tests before were not counted again: 1,464 is 31.3's
  figure.
- **L178, L200 say what the carried-over code already met — saved time:** 1 of the 4 new specs passed at once (The
  Class fetched none of the course's files and no 3D code). The rider was already alone in a class ride: the code
  that could make other riders was there, and a class never asked for them.
- **L213 see a spec fail after its code — saved time:** the first-frame rule was written with the scene's driver, so
  it was taken out and the drawing spec run again: 0 m where at least 299 were expected, then put back.
- **L147 remove from the outside in — cost time:** the `api` contracts and the `world` files were removed before the
  new page compiled, so the workspace did not build for a long stretch and the red-to-green of slices 1 and 2 came
  in one step. Outside in (page, then components, then library, then contracts) would have kept it building.
- **L148 a spec that used the removed feature as its vehicle keeps its assertion — saved time:** always solo, the
  ride's address, the initial bundle, The Class's choice, Class Ride as designed and the Ibiza holds each kept what
  they assert and now enter the ride with the course's files held.
- **L223 ride the course in the smallest window — saved time:** one spec draws the course, at 844 × 390, pinned at
  its lowest step; every other spec holds the course's files and draws nothing. Verify's specs took 36 s, from 57 s.
- **L154, L172 the mock, then the app beside it — saved time:** `ibiza` and `ibiza-loading` at 1440 × 900,
  1024 × 768 and 390 × 844; nothing differed but the readouts the fake bike does not send.
- **L190 repeated runs under `CI=1` — cost a rerun:** `--grep @critical --repeat-each 3`, 153 of 153; the new file
  `--repeat-each 10`, 40 of 40; the seven new and changed files, 509 of 510 at the first run (L239), then 510 of 510.
- **L25, L227, L234 batches from a file, written at the end — saved time:** one anchor failed in the L2 batch and
  nothing had been written.
- **L150, L196, L217 the film — saved time:** nine scenes, twelve pictures; CRF 23 gave 15.3 MB for 2:19, over the
  limit, and CRF 25 gave 12.4 MB.
- **L218 a picture on a paused clock — did not apply:** the pictures of the course are on the running clock.
- **L216 — did not apply:** `origin/main` had not moved.

**What slowed it down**

- The workspace did not build between removing the contracts and finishing the new page (L147 above).
- The phone's 48 px target for Full screen lived in the stylesheet of the page that was replaced (L237).
- The still's new folder matched a spec's pattern (L238).
- The recorder's service worker fetched the course behind the fixture's back (L236).
- The first still had a light behind the rider's head (L240).

**Decisions**

- **One ride, two boxes (ADR 0047):** `ClassRideSession` is merged into `ClassRidePage`; the store reads the way to
  ride and starts at once; `wz-class-course` or `wz-class-video` fills the box. A ride in Ibiza never creates a
  video player.
- **The library loads from a directive, not `@defer`:** `CourseSceneHost` imports `@telo/world` and creates
  `CourseScene` with `setInput`; `@defer` would have put its runtime in the initial bundle.
- **The first frame takes the clock's second** in `SceneDriver` (L2-175 AC3), proved by a unit test, by the scene's
  unit test and by the renderer's report (`firstMetres`) in the spec. The placement through the ride stays 37.2's.
- **The test hook keeps its name** (`window.__wzWorldRide`), and its report gained `riders`, `firstMetres` and
  `metres`. It is read only by the lazily loaded renderer.
- **Music:** all 192 tracks went; a ride in Ibiza is silent until 37.5 adds _Beautiful Boom Bap_. Row 37.5 said the
  deep house track "goes" there; it went here, and the row says so.
- **`public/machines/` stays**, against the prompt: Connect Bike shows `machines/echelon-ex5s.jpg`.
- **`ClassDefinition.world` is removed:** one course, which the class does not name.
- **Open question 20** closed as built; **21** closed with a still from the running app, about twenty seconds in,
  drawn with WebGL 2 in software; **26** closed as not reproduced: six frames at 1440 × 900 over 75 seconds show the
  terrace and the beams at the top right and no separate rectangle. No graphics card was used, and nothing in the
  drawing was changed.
- **Left for their slices:** the Content Security Policy still allows the map tiles' host, `blob:` and
  `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (35.1); `/music/*` stays in the static host's asset folders (37.5); `tools/course-bench` does
  not run, as before.
- **Not done:** the design system's component pages were not extended, only `components.css` (block
  `class-course`); the designs' diagrams were not redrawn, since none names what changed.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- Deleted with World loading, the behaviour they proved being gone: `class-world-loading.spec.ts` (2 specs: the
  loading screen's steps and words), `class-world-build.spec.ts` (1: the build step's progress) and
  `class-world-start.spec.ts` (6: Start riding, the handed-over renderer, the inset video and the course's music),
  with `pages/world-loading.page.ts` and `fixtures/world-music.ts`. What they proved of the course itself is in the
  new file.
- New: `class-ride-ibiza.spec.ts`, 4 specs, one `@critical`.
- Changed, none weakened: `always-solo`, `ride-address`, `the-class` (the still's address is no file of the
  course), `initial-bundle` (the 3D code arrives when a ride in Ibiza starts, and never with the class video),
  `class-ride-screen` (in Ibiza the box is measured with the still in it), `class-ride-ibiza-holds` (the clock is
  paused ten seconds ahead, L239). The heap check `heap/enter-and-leave.heap.ts` was kept compiling and not run.
- Unit tests deleted with their code: the worlds', the downloader's, World loading's view and stepping, and the
  plan's `outline` and `structure` lines.

- The wrong requirement number for the lost network went into four files (L235).

- **A video that has not played for 10 seconds of the ride cannot play** (`VideoStallWatch`, unit-tested by row), in
  the ride's own seconds; the notice goes when it plays again. `PlayerState` gained `buffering`.
- **"Refuses control"** is any answer but Success, no answer in 3 seconds, or no control point; one pure rule,
  `resistanceControl(ble, refused)`, gives `canControl` and `byHand`, one unit test per row.
- **Once refused, nothing more is written in that ride:** the controller asks the connection nothing and has it
  forget the kept target, so a reconnect does not ask for control a second time.
- **Each notice is a `role="status"` on the screen,** said once because it is drawn once; the app's live region does
  not repeat it (36.3 owns announcements).
- **Both notices sit `space-2` from what they speak of,** in the mocks, the design system's `components.css` and the
  app, so that the two together fit 844 × 390.
- **Left as it was, and written as open (L2 open question 29):** with both at once the video's notice still says "The
  bike will still follow the class."; a rider setting the resistance by hand is not told the level each segment asks
  for; a bike that refused is not asked again until the next ride.
- **Not done:** the design system's component pages were not extended; only `components.css` gained the rule.
- **No ADR:** nothing architectural was decided.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- Deleted: none. Weakened: none.

**Applied from earlier**

  started for the film was stopped by its process id, and nothing was killed by name or port.

  390 × 844. No difference but Power and Cadence, which read a dash once a silent bike has stopped reporting.
- **L213 see a spec fail after its code — saved time:** the two "no command after" specs and the spec in Ibiza were
  written with or after their code, and were seen red by taking the change out of the build and putting it back.
- **L218 a picture on a paused clock — saved time:** `onPausedClock` copied into the film's `shots.ts`.
- **L223 specs that ride the course — saved time:** one new spec rides it, once, at 844 × 390, never resized: it

- **L147, L148 remove from the outside in — did not apply:** nothing was removed but a hidden paragraph; no
  confirmation on Leave was left to delete.

  `WorkoutEngine.resend()` as this slice's.
- **L150, L186, L196, L217 the film — saved time:** three files copied, eight scenes rewritten, eighteen shots in
  30 s; CRF 23 gave 14.8 MB for 2:19. **L216 — did not apply:** `origin/main` had not moved.

**What slowed it down**

- The lost-bike alert lay over the header, so "Leave stays active" was true for a keyboard and false for a finger
  (L224).
- The fake bike's log arrived late after two minutes of keepalives (L225).
- A signal named like a private method stopped the spec server (L228).
- A batch of doc edits sent as a heredoc was sent twice (L227).
- The film's scene of leaving and finishing overlapped its own cards and was laid out again (L229).

**Decisions**

- **A screen reader hears "Ride paused" and "Ride resumed"** (L2 open question 28), through the app's one live
  region; the hidden `role="status"` paragraph that said the pause a second time, and never the resume, is gone.
- **The header is above the lost-bike alert,** in the mock and the app: Leave and Full screen take a press, and
  the alert covers the ride under the header. L2-092 AC2 stood over the mock.
- **One helper gives the ride's state** (`rideHold`, four rows, unit-tested): the button's word, whether it is
  disabled, the overlay and the alert. "Paused" is drawn for the rider's own pause alone, as the mock has it.
- **The segment's level follows the kept target after a reconnect** (`WorkoutEngine.resend()`), so a nudge does
  not survive a lost bike, as the design said.
- **No command after a ride:** `BikeConnection.forgetTarget()`, called on leaving and on finishing. The carried-over
  connection sent the ended ride's level to a bike that reconnected on The Class or on the result.
- **In Ibiza** the same overlay covers the whole box, the inset video with it, and the alert says the ride is
  paused. Two mocks drew it first, with the course's still in the box until 37.1.
- **Left as it was, and written as open:** a rider who leaves while the bike is lost keeps the connection's retries
  on The Class, where L2-017 AC1 reads "until … the rider leaves the ride"; the ride holds within a second of the
  drop, not at once; a finished ride opens the carried-over result at `/results` until journey 32.
- **No ADR:** nothing architectural was decided; `forgetTarget()` and `resend()` were already designed.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- Deleted: none.
- Rewritten: `class-pause-leave.spec.ts`. Its lost-bike spec asserted the carried-over words ("Your ride is paused.
  Pedal to wake the bike and it carries on from here."), which L2-092 AC1 replaces; it now asserts L2's words, the
  disabled Resume and stepper, and the two commands after Reconnect. Its Pause, Leave and finish specs kept every
  assertion and gained more. Six specs are new there, and `class-ride-ibiza-holds.spec.ts` is new with one.

**New lessons:** L224 to L229.

### 30.6 The Class in a blocked browser (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L2, L197, L204 the port — saved time:** every spec, verify and recording run used `E2E_PORT=4377`; the port was
  read before each server, the five servers started for the screenshots and the film were each stopped by their
  process id, and nothing was killed by name or by port.
- **L3 format first; L17 judge verify by its exit status — saved time:** verify passed before the first edit
  (56.8 s), after the code (50.4 s), after the rebase (46.3 s) and before the commit.
- **L25 all-or-nothing scripted edits — saved time:** every batch asserted its anchors; none wrote half.
- **L164, L165 verify first; counts from four commands — saved time:** 244 specs, 43 `@critical`, 1383 unit tests
  and 79.29 kB before; 283, 45, 1418 and 79.29 kB after. Of those, 4 specs and 14 unit tests came with the rider
  from `main` (L216): this slice added 35 specs and 21 unit tests.
- **L190 repeated runs under `CI=1` — saved time:** `--grep @critical --repeat-each 3`, 135 of 135; the two spec files
  added or changed, `--repeat-each 10`, 720 of 720.
- **L178, L200 say what the carried-over code already met — saved time:** see _Specs deleted or changed_.
- **L154, L172, L183, L201 look at the mock, then the app beside it — saved time:** as measurements, then by eye at
  1440 × 900, 1024 × 768 and 390 × 844, in both states and with the link copied. The alert agreed to the half
  pixel. The start button did not (L215).
- **L174 a paused clock stepped inside every read — saved time:** Copy link's two seconds are counted, not waited
  for, and the spec passed first time.
- **L176 `untappable(44)` — saved time:** the alert's action draws 36 px and takes touches across 44.
- **L188 grep the slice's number — saved time:** three designs and ADR 0044 named 30.6; all four were brought in
  line.
- **L150, L186, L196 the film — saved time:** three files copied, six scenes rewritten; eleven shots of the
  production build in 3.3 s; CRF 23 gave 14.7 MB for 2:04.
- **L206 a prompt's "today" — saved time:** it was right this time, and the first run showed it: 23 of 25 red.
- **L149 — no effect:** the row and L2 agreed. **L147, L148 — did not apply:** nothing was removed.

**What slowed it down**

- The alert's title and sentence ran together in the app, which only the spec of the whole block's words found
  (L214).
- The start button was 8 px shorter than its mock from lg, and had been since slice 30.2 (L215). It was found by
  measuring the mock and the app for this slice's disabled state, not by a spec.
- `origin/main` moved while the slice was being built (L216).
- Two of the film's pictures hid what the scene was about (L217), and two watches for the encode's end matched
  their own command line with `pgrep -f` and would never have ended; the background command's own notice is the
  watch.

**Decisions**

- **The alert draws its own action.** `wz-browser-notice` takes the action's words and an optional `href` and
  draws a primary link or a raised button, as `wz-connect-blocker` does (slice 29.2), where the design said it
  would project one. The design was brought in line. No ADR: nothing architectural changed.
- **A mock was drawn that L2 does not list as a gap:** `the-class/link-copied`, the copied state of L2-008 AC3,
  which only the design system's alert page drew.
- **A browser that refuses to copy changes nothing.** The button stays "Copy link" and no second message shows;
  the address is in the address bar (`docs/specs/L2.md`, open question 25, closed).
- **The disabled start button is `disabled`,** as the mocks draw it, and takes no Tab stop; L2-171 AC5's
  "reachable by keyboard" is read as a start button that can start something (open question 25).
- **The ride's address is guarded by `blockedBrowserGuard`, not yet by `connectedBikeGuard`.** The design named
  one guard for L2-010 AC2 and L2-087 AC4. Opening the ride without a connected bike in a browser that can reach
  one is slice 31.1's, so this slice built the narrower guard, which reads the support state alone and stands
  before the guard that reconnects; 31.1 decides whether it stays.
- **Bluetooth switched off is not a blocked browser.** The Class shows no alert for it, and its start button opens
  Connect Bike, which says so (L2-018). `isBlocked()` and `browserNotice()` each have a unit test for that row.
- **The clipboard is bound on The Class's route,** not on the parent with the bike connection as the design said:
  one screen copies.
- **The start button is 64 px tall from lg, as its mock draws it,** in every state. This is a change to a
  connected rider's screen too, made here because the disabled state is drawn from the same rule.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- **Added:** `e2e/the-class-blocked-browser.spec.ts`, 25 specs, with the fixture `e2e/fixtures/clipboard.ts` and

  `browser-notice.spec.ts`, `link-copy.spec.ts`, `blocked-browser.guard.spec.ts` and one case of

**Left for later**

Not a slice of `detailed-journeys.md`: the owner asked for Telo Ride's new course rider (`wattzup` commit
`b118c5ee6`, its ADR 0036). No percentage changed.

  bytes), byte for byte.

  was. `rider-body.ts`, `rider-turn.ts` and `rider-turn.spec.ts` are new. `course-renderer.ts` had diverged (races
  removed): the commit's five changes were made by hand, and the body is also let go of in `dispose`.
- ADR 0045; L2-174 AC11 (the rider's shape), L2-175 AC1 and AC2 (the rider's file is one of the course's), L2-177
  AC6 (its two finenesses); `/riders/*` in the host's `exclude`, in ADR 0043 and in open question 19.

**What was left**

- The other riders' coarser body: the carried-over code that makes other riders now calls the same kit, and Telo
  Ride 1 gives it none (L2-174 AC6). Nothing was added to it.
- `e2e/race-course.spec.ts` is gone with races: its assertion is in `class-world-start.spec.ts`.
- Telo Ride's detailed design (`race/ride-a-course`): riding in Ibiza has no design here yet.
- `ngsw-config.json`: its asset groups do not name `courses`, so `riders` was not added.
- `rider.py`'s first line still names Telo Ride's ADR 0036 and L2-166 AC26. The script was not run again.

**Applied from earlier**

- **L2, L197 the port — saved time:** `E2E_PORT=4381` for every run; the look's own servers on 4391 and 4392, each
  stopped by its process id.
- **L3, L17 format first, verify by its exit status — made it worse once:** verify was run before prettier and
  stopped at the format step on `static-host.spec.ts`; the second run exited 0.
- **L164, L165 counts from four commands — saved time:** 228 specs, 41 `@critical`, 1365 unit tests and 79.29 kB
  before; 232, 41, 1379 and 79.29 kB after.
- **L190 repeated runs under `CI=1` — saved time:** `--grep @critical --repeat-each 3`, 123 of 123; the two changed
  spec files `--repeat-each 5`, 80 of 80.
- **L200 say what the carried-over code already met — saved time:** of the four new specs, `/riders/no-such.glb`
  passed before the change (L212); the other three and the configuration's `exclude` failed first.

**What slowed it down**

- `wattzup`'s checked-out `main` is behind the commit, so its folder has no rider (L210).
- The production build ignores `?simulate`, and a new rider is sent to Connect Bike (L211).
- The ride in Ibiza draws a pale box the size of the video at the top right of the course. No element of the page is there,
  and the build before this change draws it too (L213): it is not the rider's, and it is not fixed
  here.

**New lessons:** L210 to L213.

  while it is held, and it is answered as `model/gltf-binary` and the course drawn; in `static-host.spec.ts`,
  `/riders/rider.glb` is served as a file, `/riders/no-such.glb` and `/riders/no-such-rider` are 404, and the
  configuration excludes the asset folders. `the-class.spec.ts` now also counts a request under `/riders/` as
  loading the course. `rider-turn.spec.ts` came with the port (14 unit tests).

**The look.** Headless Chromium drew the course with WebGL 2 (three's fallback; the probe reported `webgl2`). At
0:12 the rider is on the centre line, upright, seen from behind, hands on the hoods, one foot down and one up, and
the feet change places between two pictures 0.3 s apart. Beside the old build's picture the seat runs into the
thighs and the jersey has a hem.

### 30.5 Open where the rider belongs (2026-11-23)

**Applied from earlier**

- **L2, L197, L204 the port — saved time:** every spec, verify and recording run used `E2E_PORT=4377`; the port was
  read before each server, the one server started for the film was stopped by its process id, and nothing was killed
  by name or by port.
- **L3 format first; L17 judge verify by its exit status — saved time:** verify passed before the first edit
  (43.9 s), after the code (50.6 s) and before the commit (43.5 s). `eslint --fix e2e` is not this repo's lint;
  `npm run lint` is.
- **L25 all-or-nothing scripted edits — saved time, and once made it worse:** every edit asserted its anchor. The
  script that filled the film's numbers asserted too much and stopped, and the encode after a `;` ran anyway (L209).
- **L164, L165 verify first; counts from four commands — saved time:** 228 specs, 41 `@critical`, 1365 unit tests
  and 79.29 kB before; 244, 43, 1383 and 79.29 kB after.
- **L190 repeated runs under `CI=1` — saved time:** `--grep @critical --repeat-each 3`, 129 of 129; the three spec
  files added or changed, `--repeat-each 10`, 200 of 200.
- **L148 a moved spec keeps its assertion — saved time:** the blocked browser's spec of The Class kept its assertion
  with a remembered bike in its set-up.
- **L200 say what the carried-over code already met — saved time:** 13 of the 18 specs first run passed at once.
- **L150, L186, L196 the film — saved time:** three files copied, six scenes rewritten; seventeen shots of the
  production build in 6.0 s; CRF 23 gave 14.2 MB for 2:04.
- **L149 the specs stand; L154 look at a screenshot — no effect:** the row and L2 agreed, and no screen changed.
- **L147 — did not apply:** nothing a rider sees was removed.

**What slowed it down**

- The prompt's picture of today was out of date (L206): `/` did not always show The Class. The carried-over guard
  already sent a new rider to Connect Bike where the browser has Web Bluetooth, and let a blocked browser through to
  The Class. What the slice had to build was smaller than planned for: the blocked browser, the finished ride's
  contract, the wait for an advertisement and the smaller record.
- The fake Bluetooth had no `watchAdvertisements()`, no way to lose `getDevices()` and one identifier for every
  bike (L207).
- The film was encoded three times for one film (L209).

**Decisions**

- **"Has finished a ride" is a contract of its own until the record.** `FINISHED_RIDES` (`api`, one method,
  `any()`) is bound to `NoFinishedRides` (`domain`), which always answers no. The rule itself is the pure function
  `openingScreen()` with a case for each of the four rows, and the guard's unit test runs the four rows with the
  contract stubbed, so slice 32.1 only binds the token to the record and deletes `NoFinishedRides`
  (`docs/specs/L2.md`, open question 24, closed). No ADR: nothing architectural changed.
- **The guard no longer reads whether the browser is blocked.** A new rider in a blocked browser goes to Connect
  Bike, which says why (L2-167 AC3); The Class in a blocked browser, for a rider who has a bike, is slice 30.6. The
  same guard still stands on the class ride's address until slice 31.1, so a new rider who opens it lands on
  Connect Bike in a blocked browser too.
- **"As soon as it advertises" is `watchAdvertisements()`.** The connection waits on the permitted device, reading
  `idle`, and connects on the first advertisement; the wait ends when the rider pairs another bike, on
  `disconnect()` and on destroy. A browser without `watchAdvertisements()` keeps the one attempt at once.
- **The record is `{ id, name }`.** `identifier`, `kind` and `seenAt` are no longer written, and `lastSeen()` left
  the contract with them: nothing read it. The identifier and the protocol are read from the bike when it
  reconnects. A record an earlier version wrote is still read.
- **In the specs the default is a new rider.** The shared fixture remembers no bike and no ride: a spec that opens
  `/` with nothing set up gets Connect Bike, and a spec that needs The Class says so with
  `bluetooth.remember(echelonBike)`, as 24 spec files and `fixtures/screens.ts` already did.

**Specs deleted or changed**

- **Added:** `e2e/where-the-app-opens.spec.ts`, 13 specs, with the page object `e2e/pages/first-screen.ts`, which
  records every screen drawn while the app opens; four specs in `e2e/remembered-bike.spec.ts`; and the unit tests
  `opening-screen.spec.ts`, `remembered-bike.guard.spec.ts` and `web-bluetooth-remembered-bike.spec.ts`. Two of the
  new specs are `@critical`: a new rider on a phone and a remembered bike on a desktop.
- **Failed first, for the expected reason:** a new rider in a browser without Web Bluetooth and on plain http (The
  Class opened); a bike switched off, then on (never reconnected); what is kept, and a different bike (the record
  held `identifier`, `kind` and `seenAt` too).
- **Passed on carried-over code:** the other 13 of the first run: a new rider on Connect Bike at four widths and
  from "Back to the class"; a remembered bike on The Class at four widths, switched off, and on to Connect Bike by
  keyboard; a browser without `getDevices()`; and the reconnection that was already `@critical`.
- **Set-up changed, assertion kept:** `e2e/browser-support.spec.ts`, one spec: The Class in a browser without Web
  Bluetooth now opens for a rider who paired a bike before. It is the only spec file whose set-up changed.
- **Moved:** "a rider with no remembered bike starts on Connect Bike" left `remembered-bike.spec.ts` for
  `where-the-app-opens.spec.ts`, where it runs at four widths.
- **Rewritten with the contract:** `remembered-bike-store.spec.ts` lost its five cases of `lastSeen()` and of the
  `kind` and `identifier` a record no longer holds, and gained the record's two fields, a replaced bike and an
  earlier version's record. The fixture's `remember()` lost `lastSeenDaysAgo`, which no spec passed.
- None was weakened to pass.

**New lessons**

- **L206 to L209** are in the table above.
